 = 375.0... wait, m=30, s=1: hourDeg = 360 + (30/60)*30 = 375. previous = 375. target = 375 + floor(375/360)*360 = 375 + 360 = 735. Spin! So even if you load during the 12 o'clock hour, the hour hand spins
 every second from the first tick. Consistent with pass 1.

 ```js
   const { spawn } = require('child_process');
   const path = require('path');

   const CHROME = 'C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe';
   const PAGE = 'file:///C:/Users/default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1/Desktop/Jack%20Kernel/analog-clock/index_medium.html';

   async function main() {
     const sizes = [[1280, 800], [800, 280], [800, 306]];
     for (const [w, h] of sizes) {
       const chrome = spawn(CHROME, [
         '--headless=new',
         '--no-first-run',
         '--no-default-browser-check',
         '--disable-background-timer-throttling',
         `--window-size=${w},${h}`,
         `--remote-debugging-port=0`,
         PAGE
       ], { stdio: 'ignore' });
       // With port 0, the actual port is printed to stderr. Hmm, that's a pain.
     }
   }
 ```

 With --remote-debugging-port=0, Chrome prints "DevTools listening on ws://127.0.0.1:PORT/..." to stderr. I can capture stderr and parse it. Or just use a fixed port and run each size sequentially, killing

 Script outline:

 ```js
   const { spawn } = require('child_process');

   const CHROME = 'C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe';
   const PAGE = 'file:///C:/Users/default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1/Desktop/Jack%20Kernel/analog-clock/index_medium.html';
   const PORT = 9333;

   const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));

   async function getTargets() {
     for (let i = 0; i < 50; i++) {
       try {
         const res = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/json/list`);
         const list = await res.json();
         const page = list.find(t => t.type === 'page' && t.url.startsWith('file://'));
         if (page) return page;
       } catch (e) {}
       await sleep(200);
     }
     throw new Error('no target');
   }

   async function evaluate(wsUrl, expr) {
     const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
     await new Promise((resolve, reject) => { ws.onopen = resolve; ws.onerror = reject; });
     const id = 1;
     const result = await new Promise((resolve) => {
       const handler = (ev) => {
         const msg = JSON.parse(ev.data);
         if (msg.id === id) { ws.close(); resolve(msg.result); }
       };
       ws.onmessage = handler;
       ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method: 'Runtime.evaluate', params: { expression: expr, returnByValue: true } }));
     });
     return result;
   }

   async function measure(w, h) {
     const chrome = spawn(CHROME, [
       '--headless=new', '--no-first-run', '--no-default-browser-check',
       `--window-size=${w},${h}`, `--remote-debugging-port=${PORT}`,
       '--disable-gpu', '--no-sandbox',
       PAGE
     ], { stdio: 'ignore' });
     try {
       const target = await getTargets();
       const expr = `(() => {
         const date = document.getElementById('date');
         const title = document.getElementById('title');
         const clock = document.querySelector('.clock');
         const before = window.scrollY;
         window.scrollTo(0, 99999);
         const after = window.scrollY;
         return {
           innerHeight: window.innerHeight,
           innerWidth: window.innerWidth,
           docScrollHeight: document.documentElement.scrollHeight,
           bodyScrollHeight: document.body.scrollHeight,
           scrollY_before: before,
           scrollY_after_scrollTo: after,
           title: title.getBoundingClientRect(),
           clock: clock.getBoundingClientRect(),
           date: date.getBoundingClientRect(),
           dateText: date.textContent
         };
       })()`;
       const res = await evaluate(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl, expr);
       return res.result.value;
     } finally {
       chrome.kill();
       await sleep(500);
     }
   }

   (async () => {
     for (const [w, h] of [[1280, 800], [800, 300], [800, 250]]) {
       const m = await measure(w, h);
       console.log(`=== viewport ${w}x${h} ===`);
       console.log(JSON.stringify(m, null, 2));
     }
     process.exit(0);
   })().catch(e => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
 ```

 const { spawn } = require('child_process');

 const CHROME = 'C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe';
 const PAGE = 'file:///C:/Users/default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1/Desktop/Jack%20Kernel/analog-clock/index_medium.html';
 const PORT = 9333;

 const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));

 async function getTarget() {
   for (let i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
     try {
       const res = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/json/list`);
       const list = await res.json();
       const page = list.find(t => t.type === 'page' && t.url.startsWith('file://'));
       if (page) return page;
     } catch (e) {}
     await sleep(200);
   }
   throw new Error('no page target found');
 }

 function openWS(wsUrl) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
     const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
     ws.onopen = () => resolve(ws);
     ws.onerror = reject;
   });
 }

 async function evaluate(ws, expr) {
   const id = Math.floor(Math.random() * 1e9);
   const result = await new Promise((resolve) => {
     const handler = (ev) => {
       const msg = JSON.parse(ev.data);
       if (msg.id === id) { ws.removeEventListener('message', handler); resolve(msg); }
     };
     ws.addEventListener('message', handler);
     ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method: 'Runtime.evaluate', params: { expression: expr, returnByValue: true } }));
   });
   return result.result ? result.result.value : null;
 }

 const EXPR = `(() => {
   const date = document.getElementById('date');
   const title = document.getElementById('title');
   const clock = document.querySelector('.clock');
   const before = window.scrollY;
   window.scrollTo(0, 99999);
   const after = window.scrollY;
   const r = (el) => { const b = el.getBoundingClientRect(); return {top: Math.round(b.top), bottom: Math.round(b.bottom), height: Math.round(b.height)}; };
   return {
     innerWidth: window.innerWidth,
     innerHeight: window.innerHeight,
     docScrollHeight: document.documentElement.scrollHeight,
     bodyScrollHeight: document.body.scrollHeight,
     scrollY_before: before,
     scrollY_after_scrollTo99999: after,
     title: r(title), clock: r(clock), date: r(date),
     dateText: date.textContent
   };
 })()`;

 async function measure(w, h) {
   const chrome = spawn(CHROME, [
     '--headless=new', '--no-first-run', '--no-default-browser-check',
     '--disable-gpu', '--no-sandbox',
     `--window-size=${w},${h}`,
     `--remote-debugging-port=${PORT}`,
     PAGE
   ], { stdio: 'ignore' });
   try {
     const target = await getTarget();
     let val = null;
     for (let i = 0; i < 15; i++) {
       const ws = await openWS(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
       val = await evaluate(ws, EXPR);
       ws.close();
       if (val && val.dateText) break;
       await sleep(400);
     }
     return val;
   } finally {
     chrome.kill();
     await sleep(800);
   }
 }

 (async () => {
   for (const [w, h] of [[1280, 800], [800, 300], [800, 250], [400, 320]]) {
     const m = await measure(w, h);
     console.log(`=== viewport ${w}x${h} ===`);
     console.log(JSON.stringify(m));
   }
   process.exit(0);
 })().catch(e => { console.error('ERROR:', e.message); process.exit(1); });

 const { spawn } = require('child_process');
 const CHROME = 'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe';
 const PORT = 9334;
 const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));
 async function getTarget() {
   for (let i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
     try {
       const res = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/json/list`);
       const list = await res.json();
       const page = list.find(t => t.type === 'page' && t.url.startsWith('file://'));
       if (page) return page;
     } catch (e) {}
     await sleep(200);
   }
   throw new Error('no page target found');
 }
 function openWS(wsUrl) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => { const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl); ws.onopen = () => resolve(ws); ws.onerror = reject; });
 }
 async function evaluate(ws, expr) {
   const id = Math.floor(Math.random() * 1e9);
   const msg = await new Promise((resolve) => {
     const handler = (ev) => { const m = JSON.parse(ev.data); if (m.id === id) { ws.removeEventListener('message', handler); resolve(m); } };
     ws.addEventListener('message', handler);
     ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method: 'Runtime.evaluate', params: { expression: expr, returnByValue: true } }));
   });
   return (msg.result && msg.result.result) ? msg.result.result.value : null;
 }
 const EXPR = `(() => {
   window.scrollTo(0, 99999);
   return { innerHeight: window.innerHeight, docScrollHeight: document.documentElement.scrollHeight, scrollY_after: window.scrollY };
 })()`;
 async function measure(file, w, h) {
   const chrome = spawn(CHROME, ['--headless=new','--no-first-run','--no-default-browser-check','--disable-gpu','--no-sandbox',
     `--window-size=${w},${h}`, `--remote-debugging-port=${PORT}`, `file:///${file.replace(/\\/g,'/')}`], { stdio: 'ignore' });
   try {
     const target = await getTarget();
     const ws = await openWS(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     const val = await evaluate(ws, EXPR);
     ws.close();
     return val;
   } finally { chrome.kill(); await sleep(800); }
 }
 (async () => {
   const base = 'C:/Users/default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1/AppData/Local/Temp/clock_audit_p5';
   for (const f of ['control_body_hidden.html','control_html_hidden.html','control_visible.html']) {
     const m = await measure(base + '/' + f, 800, 300);
     console.log(f, JSON.stringify(m));
   }
   process.exit(0);
 })().catch(e => { console.error('ERROR:', e.message); process.exit(1); });
 EOF

 $ cat > /tmp/clock_audit_p5/measure3.js <<'EOF'
 const { spawn } = require('child_process');
 const CHROME = 'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe';
 const PORT = 9335;
 const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));
 async function getTarget() {
   for (let i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
     try {
       const res = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/json/list`);
       const list = await res.json();
       const page = list.find(t => t.type === 'page' && t.url.startsWith('file://'));
       if (page) return page;
     } catch (e) {}
     await sleep(200);
   }
   throw new Error('no page target found');
 }
 function openWS(wsUrl) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => { const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl); ws.onopen = () => resolve(ws); ws.onerror = reject; });
 }
 let nextId = 1;
 function send(ws, method, params) {
   const id = nextId++;
   return new Promise((resolve) => {
     const handler = (ev) => { const m = JSON.parse(ev.data); if (m.id === id) { ws.removeEventListener('message', handler); resolve(m); } };
     ws.addEventListener('message', handler);
     ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params: params || {} }));
   });
 }
 async function evaluate(ws, expr) {
   const m = await send(ws, 'Runtime.evaluate', { expression: expr, returnByValue: true });
   return (m.result && m.result.result) ? m.result.result.value : null;
 }
 async function run(fileUrl, w, h) {
   const chrome = spawn(CHROME, ['--headless=new','--no-first-run','--no-default-browser-check','--disable-gpu','--no-sandbox',
     `--window-size=${w},${h}`, `--remote-debugging-port=${PORT}`, fileUrl], { stdio: 'ignore' });
   try {
     const target = await getTarget();
     const ws = await openWS(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     await send(ws, 'Runtime.enable');
     // wheel scroll at center

 const { spawn } = require('child_process');
 const CHROME = 'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe';
 const PORT = 9336;
 const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));
 async function getTarget() {
   for (let i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
     try {
       const res = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/json/list`);
       const list = await res.json();
       const page = list.find(t => t.type === 'page' && t.url.startsWith('file://'));
       if (page) return page;
     } catch (e) {}
     await sleep(200);
   }
   throw new Error('no page target found');
 }
 function openWS(wsUrl) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => { const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl); ws.onopen = () => resolve(ws); ws.onerror = reject; });
 }
 let nextId = 1;
 function send(ws, method, params) {
   const id = nextId++;
   return new Promise((resolve) => {
     const handler = (ev) => { const m = JSON.parse(ev.data); if (m.id === id) { ws.removeEventListener('message', handler); resolve(m); } };
     ws.addEventListener('message', handler);
     ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params: params || {} }));
   });
 }
 async function evaluate(ws, expr) {
   const m = await send(ws, 'Runtime.evaluate', { expression: expr, returnByValue: true });
   return (m.result && m.result.result) ? m.result.result.value : null;
 }
 async function run(fileUrl, w, h) {
   const chrome = spawn(CHROME, ['--headless=new','--no-first-run','--no-default-browser-check','--disable-gpu','--no-sandbox',
     `--window-size=${w},${h}`, `--remote-debugging-port=${PORT}`, fileUrl], { stdio: 'ignore' });
   try {
     const target = await getTarget();
     const ws = await openWS(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     await send(ws, 'Runtime.enable');
     await send(ws, 'Input.dispatchMouseEvent', { type: 'mouseWheel', x: Math.floor(w/2), y: Math.floor(h/2), deltaX: 0, deltaY: 800 });
     await sleep(300);
     const val = {
       file: fileUrl.split('/').pop(),
       scrollY_after_wheel: await evaluate(ws, 'window.scrollY'),
       docScrollHeight: await evaluate(ws, 'document.documentElement.scrollHeight'),
       innerHeight: await evaluate(ws, 'window.innerHeight')
     };
     ws.close();
     return val;
   } finally { chrome.kill(); await sleep(800); }
 }

 Pass 5 Handoff — Analog Clock Forensic Audit

 Review Category

 Interfaces, data integrity, integration, configuration, maintainability, and testability — specifically layout/viewport-fit robustness (content reachability on short viewports).

 Primary Finding

 Finding ID: AC-P5-01
 Severity: LOW
 Status: CONFIRMED

 Exact location: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html, line 28 — overflow: hidden; inside the body rule (lines 21–33). (The other overflow: hidden at line 65, on
 .clock-face, is intentional circular clipping and is not part of this finding.)

 Observed behavior (runtime-verified, Chrome/Blink 152 headless):
 - The page's flex column has a fixed total content height of 299px (measured: title 36px + gap 22px + clock 200px + gap 22px + date 19px; 292px at narrow widths where the title clamp() shrinks).
 - On any viewport shorter than that (e.g., measured innerHeight 149px at an 800×300 headless window; any desktop window with inner height < ~300px), document.documentElement.scrollHeight (299) exceeds
   innerHeight (149), yet user-initiated scrolling does nothing: a dispatched wheel event (deltaY 800) left window.scrollY at 0.
 - Pre-scroll layout at that viewport: title y=0..36, clock y=58..258, date line y=280..299 — entirely below the fold. With scrolling disabled, the date line and the bottom ~109px of the clock face are
   clipped and permanently unreachable (no scrollbar appears to hint at more content).

 Expected behavior: When content exceeds the viewport, the document should scroll (or the layout should shrink to fit), so the date line and full clock face remain reachable/visible.

 Root cause: overflow: hidden on body — with no html overflow rule (html is visible by default) — is propagated to the viewport per CSS Overflow §3 (viewport overflow rule: when the root HTML element computes
 to visible, the body's overflow value is applied to the viewport). This removes the only mechanism (scrolling) that would make overflowing content reachable, while the layout assumes the viewport is always ≥
 ~300px tall (fixed 200px clock + fixed 22px gaps + text lines, centered via min-height: 100vh + justify-content: center).

 Evidence:
 1. Code: line 28 overflow: hidden in body rule; grep -n "html\s*{" → no html rule (html overflow = default visible).
 2. Runtime on the actual page (viewport 784×149 CSS): docScrollHeight=299 > innerHeight=149; wheel event → scrollY stays 0 (non-scrollable); bounding rects: date {top:280, bottom:299} entirely below the
    149px fold.
 3. Controls (same engine, same viewport): page with no overflow rule → wheel scrolls (scrollY 0→800); page with html{overflow:hidden} → wheel blocked (scrollY 0). This isolates the block to the overflow rule
    and proves the wheel mechanism itself works.
 4. Screenshot captured at 800×300 (short_viewport.png in temp dir) shows the clipped bottom.
 5. Caveat (evidence boundary): in this headless build, programmatic window.scrollTo bypasses the viewport's overflow:hidden (observed scrollY 0→150) — a headless quirk, not page behavior. User-initiated
    scrolling (wheel) is the valid signal, and it is blocked. In real browsers the scrollbar simply never appears and wheel/trackpad/keyboard scrolling does nothing.

 Impact assessment (why LOW, not MEDIUM): Trigger is an edge case (viewport inner height < ~292–299px: small desktop windows, split panes, some low-res setups). Consequence is limited: the secondary date line
 (and lower clock portion) is silently unreachable; the primary time display remains visible for viewports ≥ ~160px; trivially worked around by resizing the window. No data, security, or primary-workflow
 failure.

 Repair Direction

 Remove overflow: hidden; from the body rule (line 28) so the document scrolls normally whenever content exceeds the viewport. Alternatives (equally valid, more work): make the column fit short viewports —
 e.g., responsive clock size (width: min(200px, 55vmin) with matching height), reduced gap, or overflow-y: auto on html/body. The minimal robust fix is deleting line 28; nothing else on the page depends on
 it.

 Preservation constraints:
 - Keep .clock-face { overflow: hidden } (line 65) — it intentionally clips hands to the circular face.
 - Keep min-height: 100vh + justify-content: center so the layout stays centered on tall viewports (no layout shift when content fits).
 - Do not alter hand/face/mark geometry or any visual styling.
 - Do not conflate with Pass 3's missing-viewport-meta fix — independent mechanisms (scroll clipping vs. mobile scale); the two fixes are complementary, not conflicting.

 Post-patch verification (for the work agent):
 1. Open the page in Chrome with a window inner height < ~300px (e.g., 800×250): the date line must be fully visible/reachable — a scrollbar appears (or content fits) and wheel/trackpad scrolling reaches the
    bottom. Headless CDP check: dispatch mouseWheel (deltaY > 0) → window.scrollY increases; date.getBoundingClientRect().bottom becomes ≤ scrollY + innerHeight.
 2. At 1280×800: layout must be pixel-identical to pre-patch (content centered, no scrollbar, title/clock/date positions unchanged).
 3. Confirm the clock face still clips its hands (second-hand tip remains inside the face; no visual change to the dial).

 Compact Prior-Finding Registry (duplicate avoidance only)

 - Pass 1 (AC-P1): Functional correctness — MEDIUM, CONFIRMED — index_medium.html: hour hand spins 360°/sec throughout the 12 o'clock hour (entry 12:00:01, recovery 13:00:00); root cause getHours() % 12 || 12
    + monotonic-angle logic.
 - Pass 2 (AC-P2): State/lifecycle — LOW, CONFIRMED — sole ~1s setTimeout chain (line 243) with no visibility/lifecycle handling → stale display when hidden, fast-spin catch-up on return.
 - Pass 3 (AC-P3): Configuration/portability — LOW, CONFIRMED — missing <meta name="viewport"> → mobile renders at ~1/5 scale, date unreadable.
 - Pass 4 (AC-P4-01): Accessibility — LOW, CONFIRMED — time conveyed only by rotated empty divs; no role/aria-label/text representation.

 Out-of-Scope / Residual Observations (durable for final report only; not new findings)

 - The file is named index_medium.html, not index.html — it will not auto-load when the folder is opened in a browser (trivial portability nit).
 - <title> is "Analog Clock" while the <h1> reads "Jack Kernel Demo" (cosmetic mismatch).
 - The four .mark elements carry unused data-hour attributes (dead data).
 - On load, the hands animate from 0° to the current time over the 50ms transition (one-time, barely perceptible).
 - The midnight (0 o'clock hour) hour-hand spin is the same root cause as Pass 1's noon window (|| 12 maps both 0 and 12 to 12), so it belongs to the Pass 1 finding, not a separate defect.
 - In this headless Chrome build, programmatic window.scrollTo bypasses viewport overflow:hidden (headless quirk, not a page defect).

 The recommended repair specification is self-contained within the single HTML file and requires no external dependencies.

 Finding 1 — Hour-Hand Runaway Spin During the 12 O’Clock Hour

 Finding ID: Pass 1 / ANALOG-P1-001 / AC-P1
 Review Category: Functional correctness and runtime behavior
 Severity: MEDIUM
 Status: CONFIRMED — static code evidence plus non-mutating simulation of the exact logic.

 ### Exact Location

 File: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html

 - Line 222:
   ```js
     const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12; // 12-hour format, 0 -> 12
   ```

 - Line 227:
   ```js
     const hourDeg = (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;
   ```
   For h = 12, this yields:
   ```text
     360° + (m / 60) * 30
   ```
   which is in the range:
   ```text
     [360°, 389.5°)
   ```
   instead of:
   ```text
     [0°, 29.5°)
   ```

 - Lines 201–210, applyRotation(key, hand, targetDeg):
     - Line 206:
       ```js
         target += Math.floor(previous / 360) * 360;
       ```
     - Line 207:
       ```js
         while (target < previous) { target += 360; }
       ```

   This logic is only correct when the incoming targetDeg < 360.

 - Line 99:
   ```css
     .hand { transition: transform 0.05s ease-out; }
   ```
   This makes each unforced +360° step render as a visible 50 ms full rotation.

 - Line 231:
   ```js
     applyRotation('hour', hourHand, hourDeg);
   ```
   This is the affected call.

 ### Observed Behavior

 Using the file’s exact logic with per-second ticks:

 - 11:59:59 → 12:00:00:
     - stored hour-hand angle: 359.5° → 360°
     - delta: +0.5°
     - visually correct.
 - 12:00:01:
     - stored angle becomes 720°
     - delta: +360°
     - visible full spin.
 - 12:00:02:
     - stored angle becomes 1080°
     - delta: +360°
     - visible full spin.
 - This repeats every second through 12:59:59.
     - Approximately 3,599 ticks each receive a full +360° step.
     - Stored angle reaches roughly 1,296,030° by the end of the 12 o’clock hour.
 - 13:00:00:
     - delta returns to +0.5°
     - behavior cleanly recovers.
 - 13:00:01 onward:
     - delta is 0° per second, as expected for a minute-only hour-hand model.

 Midnight has the same defect:

 - 00:00:00 → 00:00:01:
     - 00:00:00 is visually correct.
     - 00:00:01 onward receives repeated +360° steps.
 - Recovery occurs at 01:00:00.

 A fresh page load at exactly 12:00:00 shows a correct first paint, then begins the same spinning behavior from the second tick.

 The minute and second hands are unaffected because their raw target angles remain below 360°:

 - Minute-hand maximum: approximately 359.9°.
 - Second-hand maximum: 354° per the Pass 1 simulation.
 - No backward rotations occurred in the full-day simulation.
 - No wrong modulo-360 position ever occurred in the simulation.

 ### Expected Behavior

 The hour hand should:

 - Advance +0.5° per minute.
 - Remain unchanged per second within a minute, consistent with the existing minute-only hour-hand calculation.
 - Creep through the 12 o’clock hour from the 12 position toward the 1 position.
 - Wrap from 12:59 to 1:00 with a +0.5° step.
 - Never perform a full 360° rotation except as a single visually equivalent wrap when crossing 0°, and even that should not appear as repeated full spins.

 ### Root Cause

 hourDeg is passed to applyRotation unnormalized.

 The expression:

 ```js
   now.getHours() % 12 || 12
 ```

 maps both 0 and 12 to 12. Therefore, for the 12 o’clock hour, the hour base becomes 360° instead of 0°.

 That makes the raw hour-hand target lie in:

 ```text
   [360°, 390°)
 ```

 The applyRotation wrap math assumes the incoming raw target is below 360°. It computes:

 ```js
   target += Math.floor(previous / 360) * 360;
 ```

 then only pushes the target upward while:

 ```js
   target < previous
 ```

 When the raw target is already ≥ 360°, the base-aligned target can land one full turn above previous, and the while guard accepts it. Nothing subtracts the surplus 360°. The 50 ms CSS transition then
 animates each accumulated surplus turn.

 ### Why MEDIUM and Not HIGH

 - The defect is reproducible with certainty twice daily: noon and midnight.
 - It affects the primary UI element for a full hour each occurrence.
 - It is a major visible malfunction.
 - However, the static displayed position is always correct modulo 360°.
 - The clock never presents an incorrect time.
 - No data, security, availability, or other-page impact is identified.
 - It self-recovers at 01:00:00 and 13:00:00.

 Therefore it is MEDIUM, not HIGH.

 ### Required Correction

 Normalize the hour angle into [0°, 360°) before it reaches applyRotation.

 Recommended minimal correction at lines 222/227:

 ```js
   const h12 = now.getHours() % 12;
   const hourDeg = (h12 / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;
 ```

 This maps:

 - 00:xx → hour base 0°
 - 12:xx → hour base 0°
 - 01:xx → hour base 30°
 - 11:xx → hour base 330°

 This keeps the existing applyRotation monotonic-forward semantics intact and avoids the unforced +360° overshoot.

 Alternative acceptable approach from Pass 1: normalize the incoming hour target immediately before accumulation. However, because later passes constrain changes to applyRotation, the preferred minimal repair
 is to normalize the hour angle at the source.

 ### Preservation Constraints

 - Do not alter the second-hand or minute-hand angle calculations.
 - Do not alter applyRotation’s forward-only accumulation semantics beyond any separate transition suppression required by Pass 2.
 - Preserve the 50 ms transition for normal per-second motion.
 - Preserve the 1-second boundary alignment of the timer chain.
 - Keep the page single-file and dependency-free.

 ### Post-Patch Verification

 1. Simulate or run the page through the following windows:
     - 11:59:59 → 13:00:02
     - 23:59:59 → 01:00:02
 2. Confirm:
     - At 12:00:00, hour-hand angle is visually at 0°/12 o’clock.
     - At 12:00:01, there is no +360° step.
     - Through 12:00:00–12:59:59, the hour hand does not spin.
     - At 13:00:00, the hour hand advances +0.5°.
     - Through the midnight hour, the hour hand does not spin.
     - At 01:00:00, recovery is clean.
 3. Confirm no backward rotations.
 4. Confirm modulo-360 positions remain correct at every tick.
 5. Confirm minute and second hands remain unchanged in behavior.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Finding 2 — No Visibility/Lifecycle Handling for Backgrounded Tabs

 Finding ID: Pass 2 / AC-P2
 Review Category: State, lifecycle, concurrency, async behavior, and error handling
 Severity: LOW
 Status: CONFIRMED at code level. Runtime staleness magnitude depends on browser timer-throttling behavior and was not runtime-observed on this machine.

 ### Exact Location

 File: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html

 - tick() — lines 241–244.
 - Sole timer:
   ```js
     setTimeout(tick, 1000 - (Date.now() % 1000))
   ```
   located at line 243.
 - Initial tick() call — line 246.
 - CSS transition:
   ```css
     .hand { transition: transform 0.05s ease-out; }
   ```
   line 99.
 - applyRotation() forward-only accumulation — lines 201–214.
 - No visibilitychange, requestAnimationFrame, or addEventListener anywhere in the file, verified by grep.

 ### Observed Behavior

 - The clock’s only update mechanism is a self-rescheduling approximately 1-second setTimeout chain.
 - When the tab is hidden or backgrounded, browser timer throttling can stall or drastically slow the chain.
 - The displayed time can become stale.
 - When the user returns to the tab, no immediate update path exists.
 - The stale display persists until the next throttled timer fires.
 - applyRotation then advances each hand by the shortest forward rotation, up to approximately 360°.
 - The 50 ms CSS transition animates that catch-up as a visible fast spin.

 ### Expected Behavior

 When the user returns to the visible tab, the clock should immediately display the current time.

 Large catch-up corrections should reposition the hands without an animated full-or-near-full spin.

 ### Root Cause

 1. No lifecycle hook exists.
     - The page does not listen for visibilitychange or focus.
     - There is no immediate update path when the page becomes visible again.
 2. The sole update path is exactly the mechanism browsers throttle in hidden tabs.
     - Chrome documents background-tab timer throttling, including intensive wake-up throttling after prolonged hidden/inactive periods.
     - Firefox/Safari have similar or stricter behavior.
 3. The CSS transition on .hand animates any transform change, including large catch-up jumps.

 ### Evidence

 - grep -n over the file:
     - The only timer is line 243.
     - Zero occurrences of visibilitychange, requestAnimationFrame, or addEventListener.
 - Line 99:
   ```css
     transition: transform 0.05s ease-out;
   ```
   applies to all three hands.
 - applyRotation always yields a forward target within one +360° step, so long gaps resolve as forward rotation of up to approximately 360°.
 - Non-mutating simulation showed that the HTML-spec 4 ms minimum delay for deeply nested timers is never triggered by this chain because the scheduled delay remains at least approximately 996 ms.
     - This rejects “unbounded setTimeout nesting” as a separate user-visible defect.
     - It isolates background-tab throttling as the realistic timer-lifecycle failure mode.

 ### Why LOW

 - The code-level gap is certain.
 - The runtime severity depends on browser and version.
 - Worst-case staleness is approximately one minute in documented Chrome intensive-throttling scenarios, possibly longer in stricter browsers.
 - The consequence is stale display and a visible catch-up spin, not data corruption, security failure, or unavailability.
 - The page remains usable after correction occurs.

 ### Required Correction

 1. Add immediate update on visibility restore.
     - Add:
       ```js
         document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', ...)
       ```
     - When:
       ```js
         document.visibilityState === 'visible'
       ```
       call updateClock() immediately.
     - Re-anchor the timer chain:
         - Store the pending timeout ID.
         - Clear any existing pending timeout.
         - Schedule exactly one new timer chain.
         - Ensure no duplicate timer chains survive.

 2. Suppress animated catch-up for large jumps.
     - In applyRotation, or a wrapper around it:
         - Compute the forward delta.
         - If the delta exceeds a threshold, for example 90°:
             - set:
               ```js
                 hand.style.transition = 'none';
               ```
             - assign the transform;
             - force a reflow:
               ```js
                 void hand.offsetWidth;
               ```
             - restore the transition.
     - This makes hidden-tab catch-up or system-clock jumps reposition instantly instead of spinning.

 3. Keep the change self-contained within the single file.
     - No new dependencies.

 ### Preservation Constraints

 - Do not alter the angle computations at lines 226–228 except as required by Finding 1.
 - Do not alter the forward-only accumulation semantics of applyRotation beyond transition suppression.
 - Preserve the 1-second boundary alignment:
   ```js
     1000 - (Date.now() % 1000)
   ```
 - Preserve the 50 ms transition for normal per-second motion.
 - Ensure the transition suppression only affects large jumps and does not suppress normal second/minute/hour motion.
 - Ensure the visibility handler creates exactly one pending timer chain.

 ### Post-Patch Verification

 1. Visible tab:
     - Hands advance once per second with smooth 50 ms easing.
     - Date line updates.
     - Exactly one pending timer exists at any time.
     - No stutter or skipped seconds.

 2. Background test:
     - Switch tabs or minimize the window for at least 5 minutes.
     - Return to the tab.
     - The clock should read the current time immediately.
     - There should be no visible hand spin.

 3. Forced time jump:
     - Change the system clock forward by approximately 10 minutes, or otherwise force a large jump.
     - Hands should reposition instantly.
     - Hands should land at the correct position without a 50 ms spin.

 4. Regression check:
     - If the Finding 1 hour-angle normalization is applied, confirm no regression in hour-hand behavior during 12:00:00–13:00:00.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Finding 3 — Missing Mobile Viewport Meta

 Finding ID: Pass 3 / AC-P3
 Review Category: Interfaces, data integrity, integration, configuration, maintainability, and testability — specifically configuration/portability
 Severity: LOW
 Status: CONFIRMED at file level. Mobile rendering consequence is standard browser behavior and was not observed in an actual mobile browser during this pass.

 ### Exact Location

 File: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html

 - <head> region, lines 1–6:
     - The only meta tag is:
       ```html
         <meta charset="UTF-8">
       ```
       at line 4.
     - There is no viewport meta anywhere in the file.
 - Fixed-px layout with no responsive fallbacks:
     - .clock { width: 200px; height: 200px; } — lines 47–48.
     - body { overflow: hidden; } — line 28.
     - #title font:
       ```css
         clamp(1.6rem, 4vw, 2.25rem)
       ```
       line 38.
     - #date:
       ```css
         font-size: 0.98rem;
       ```
       line 160.
 - Zero occurrences of @media, device-width, max-width, or min-width in the file.

 ### Observed Behavior — Expected from Standard Browser Defaults

 On a smartphone, for example a 390 px physical-width device:

 - With no viewport meta, mobile browsers typically default to approximately 980 CSS px layout viewport.
 - The 200 px clock renders at:
   ```text
     200 / 980 ≈ 20%
   ```
   of the screen width.
 - On a 390 px device, that is approximately 80 physical px.
 - The title clamps to approximately 2.25rem, roughly 14 physical px.
 - The date renders at approximately 6 physical px.
 - The date becomes effectively unreadable without pinch-zoom.
 - The user must pinch-zoom to use the page.

 On desktop, the page renders as intended.

 ### Expected Behavior

 On mobile, the layout viewport should equal the device width:

 ```html
   <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
 ```

 Then the 200 px clock should occupy a sensible share of a 390 px device:

 ```text
   200 / 390 ≈ 51%
 ```

 The title and date should be legible without zoom.

 ### Root Cause

 The page layout is entirely fixed-unit:

 - Clock size is fixed at 200 px.
 - Text uses rem.
 - Gaps are fixed.
 - Nothing tells mobile browsers to size the layout viewport to the device width.

 The single 4vw term in the title clamp() is evaluated against the desktop-scale layout viewport, so it does not compensate for the missing viewport meta.

 The result is a desktop-scale document painted at approximately 1/5 physical scale on phones.

 ### Evidence

 - grep -n "meta":
     - Only line 4:
       ```html
         <meta charset="UTF-8">
       ```
 - grep -ni "viewport|@media|device-width|max-width|min-width":
     - No matches.
 - Lines 47–48:
   ```css
     width: 200px;
     height: 200px;
   ```
 - Line 160:
   ```css
     font-size: 0.98rem;
   ```
 - Line 28:
   ```css
     overflow: hidden;
   ```
 - The file is the sole project artifact, so no external CSS or JS supplies viewport handling.

 ### Why LOW

 - The defect is real and verifiable.
 - It affects mobile usability.
 - It is fully recoverable by pinch-zoom.
 - Desktop rendering, likely the primary target for this local demo, is unaffected.
 - No data, security, or availability impact is identified.

 ### Required Correction

 Add to <head>, immediately after the charset meta:

 ```html
   <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
 ```

 The minimal fix is the meta tag alone.

 Optional enhancement, only if mobile legibility of the clock itself is a goal:

 ```css
   .clock {
     width: min(200px, 60vw);
     height: min(200px, 60vw);
   }
 ```

 This optional enhancement is not required to close the finding and may change small-window desktop-adjacent sizing.

 ### Preservation Constraints

 - Keep the page single-file with no external dependencies.
 - Desktop rendering must remain pixel-identical:
     - Clock exactly 200×200 px at 1:1 scale.
     - Flex-centered column unchanged.
     - Drop-shadow unchanged.
     - JS tick/angle logic untouched.
 - Do not alter overflow: hidden on body as part of this repair.
     - That is separately owned by Finding 5.
 - Do not alter hand/angle code.
     - Those are separately owned by Findings 1 and 2.

 ### Post-Patch Verification

 1. Open the file in a mobile browser or desktop DevTools device emulation, for example iPhone 390×844.
     - The clock should render approximately 200 CSS px.
     - That should be approximately 51% of screen width.
     - Title and date should be legible without zoom.
 2. Confirm:
     - No horizontal scrollbar.
     - Nothing is clipped by body { overflow: hidden; } at the emulated size.
 3. Confirm desktop rendering without emulation is unchanged:
     - .clock computes to exactly 200×200 px.
     - Hands still tick correctly once per second.
 4. Lint the head:
     - Exactly two meta tags:
         - charset
         - viewport
     - No other head changes.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Finding 4 — Time Is Not Exposed to Assistive Technology

 Finding ID: Pass 4 / AC-P4-01
 Review Category: Interfaces, data integrity, integration, configuration, maintainability, and testability — specifically accessibility
 Severity: LOW
 Status: CONFIRMED — static markup evidence is complete; no runtime needed to establish the absence of accessible time semantics.

 ### Exact Location

 File: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html

 - Line 171:
   ```html
     <div class="clock">
   ```
   The clock container has no role and no aria-label.
 - Lines 180–182:
   ```html
     #hourHand
     #minuteHand
     #secondHand
   ```
   These are empty <div> elements whose only dynamic state is an inline transform.
 - Line 217, inside applyRotation:
   ```js
     hand.style.transform = ...
   ```
 - Line 178:
   ```html
     <div class="face-label">simple clock</div>
   ```
   This is decorative text, not a time description.
 - Line 187:
   ```html
     <div id="date"></div>
   ```
   This is the only dynamic text element.
 - Line 233:
   ```js
     dateEl.textContent = ...
   ```
   Populates the date only, using toLocaleDateString, never the time.
 - Line 183:
   ```html
     aria-hidden="true"
   ```
   This is on the decorative center diamond and is the only ARIA usage in the file.

 ### Observed Behavior

 The accessibility tree exposes, for the clock region:

 - The page heading: “Jack Kernel Demo”.
 - The decorative label: “simple clock”.
 - The date line.

 It does not expose:

 - Hour.
 - Minute.
 - Second.
 - Current time.

 The hands carry no accessible name, role, or description. A screen-reader user focusing or scanning the page receives no hour/minute/second information at all, on every load, for the entire session.

 ### Expected Behavior

 The time is the page’s primary output. It should be available programmatically.

 For example:

 - The clock element should expose a time label that updates each tick.
 - Assistive technology users should be able to obtain the same time information that sighted users see.

 ### Root Cause

 The design encodes time purely as visual geometry.

 updateClock(), lines 222–238, writes time only to:

 - style.transform on the three hands.
 - dateEl.textContent, date only.

 No code path ever emits the time as text.

 The markup provides no accessible role/label scaffolding for the clock.

 The single aria-hidden="true" at line 183 shows that ARIA was used only to suppress decoration, not to expose the clock’s data.

 ### Evidence

 - grep -n "aria":
     - Only match:
         - Line 183:
           ```html
             aria-hidden="true"
           ```
 - grep -n "role=":
     - No matches.
 - grep -ni "aria-live\|sr-only\|visually-hidden":
     - No matches.
 - Lines 180–182:
     - Hand elements are empty.
     - Their only dynamic property is transform.
 - Line 233:
     - The only per-second text update is the date string.
     - It omits hours, minutes, and seconds.
 - The file is the sole project artifact, so no external script or component supplies accessibility semantics.

 ### Why LOW

 - This is a meaningful accessibility gap.
 - It prevents AT users from obtaining the page’s primary data.
 - However, it has no visual layout impact.
 - It has no data, security, or availability impact.
 - It is fixable without changing the visual design.

 ### Required Correction

 1. Give the clock element a semantic role.
     - Recommended:
       ```html
         <div class="clock" role="timer">
       ```
     - role="timer" is semantically appropriate for a clock.
     - It defaults to aria-live="off", avoiding per-second announcement spam.

 2. Update an accessible label each tick.
     - In updateClock(), alongside existing writes:
       ```js
         clockEl.setAttribute('aria-label', now.toLocaleTimeString(undefined, {
           hour: 'numeric',
           minute: '2-digit',
           second: '2-digit'
         }));
       ```
     - Optionally prefix with the date already computed, reusing the existing toLocaleDateString call.

 3. Optional redundant safety net:
     - Add a visually hidden text node updated each tick.
     - Use a standard clip pattern, for example:
       ```css
         position: absolute;
         width: 1px;
         height: 1px;
         clip-path: inset(50%);
         overflow: hidden;
         white-space: nowrap;
       ```
     - It must not affect layout.

 4. Do not use aria-live="polite" on the per-second time.
     - That would announce every second and be hostile to AT users.

 ### Preservation Constraints

 - No visual/layout change.
     - The clock must remain 200×200 px.
     - Centering must remain unchanged.
     - Existing rendering must remain pixel-identical.
 - Do not alter tick scheduling at line 243.
 - Do not alter angle math at lines 224–228 except as required by Finding 1.
 - Do not alter applyRotation normalization except as required by Finding 2’s transition suppression.
 - Keep the existing aria-hidden="true" on the center diamond.
 - Keep decorative marks non-semantic.
 - Keep the page single-file and dependency-free.

 ### Post-Patch Verification

 1. Open the page in a browser.
     - In DevTools Accessibility panel, or by querying:
       ```js
         document.querySelector('.clock').getAttribute('aria-label')
       ```
     - Confirm the clock exposes a time label.
     - Confirm the label changes each second.
     - Confirm it matches the visual hands.

 2. Verify with a screen reader, such as NVDA or VoiceOver:
     - The time is announced on focus of the clock element.
     - There is no per-second announcement stream.

 3. Visual regression:
     - Confirm no layout shift.
     - Clock remains 200×200 px and centered.
     - #date position unchanged.
     - Screenshot diff before/after should show no visual difference.

 4. If a hidden text node is used:
     - Confirm it contributes zero layout.
     - getBoundingClientRect() width/height should be approximately 1 px.
     - Body scroll size should remain unchanged.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Finding 5 — body { overflow: hidden; } Makes Short-Viewport Content Unreachable

 Finding ID: Pass 5 / AC-P5-01
 Review Category: Interfaces, data integrity, integration, configuration, maintainability, and testability — specifically layout/viewport-fit robustness
 Severity: LOW
 Status: CONFIRMED by runtime verification in headless Chrome/Blink 152.

 ### Exact Location

 File: C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html

 - Line 28:
   ```css
     overflow: hidden;
   ```
   inside the body rule, lines 21–33.
 - The other overflow: hidden at line 65 is on .clock-face and is intentional circular clipping.
     - That one is not part of this finding.

 ### Observed Behavior — Runtime-Verified

 Measured fixed content height:

 ```text
   299 px
 ```

 Breakdown:

 ```text
   title 36 px
   gap 22 px
   clock 200 px
   gap 22 px
   date 19 px
 ```

 At narrow widths where the title clamp() shrinks, the height can be approximately:

 ```text
   292 px
 ```

 On any viewport shorter than that:

 - Example measured headless window:
     - CSS viewport: 784×149
     - document.documentElement.scrollHeight: 299
     - innerHeight: 149
 - User-initiated scrolling does nothing:
     - A dispatched wheel event with deltaY 800 left window.scrollY at 0.
 - Pre-scroll layout at that viewport:
     - Title: y = 0..36
     - Clock: y = 58..258
     - Date line: y = 280..299
 - The date line is entirely below the fold.
 - With scrolling disabled, the date line and the bottom approximately 109 px of the clock face are clipped and permanently unreachable.
 - No scrollbar appears to indicate more content.

 ### Expected Behavior

 When content exceeds the viewport, the document should scroll, or the layout should shrink to fit.

 The date line and full clock face should remain reachable/visible.

 ### Root Cause

 overflow: hidden on body, with no html overflow rule, is propagated to the viewport.

 Per CSS Overflow behavior:

 - When the root html element computes to visible, the body element’s overflow value is applied to the viewport.

 This removes scrolling, which is the only mechanism that would make overflowing content reachable.

 The layout assumes the viewport is always at least approximately 300 px tall because it uses:

 - Fixed 200 px clock.
 - Fixed 22 px gaps.
 - Text lines.
 - Centering via:
   ```css
     min-height: 100vh;
     justify-content: center;
   ```

 ### Evidence

 1. Code:
     - Line 28:
       ```css
         overflow: hidden;
       ```
     - grep -n "html\s*{":
         - No html rule exists.
         - Therefore html overflow defaults to visible.

 2. Runtime on the actual page:
     - Viewport: 784×149 CSS px.
     - docScrollHeight = 299
     - innerHeight = 149
     - Wheel event → scrollY remains 0.
     - Date bounding rect:
         - top: 280
         - bottom: 299
     - Date is entirely below the 149 px fold.

 3. Controls in the same engine and viewport:
     - Page with no overflow rule:
         - Wheel scrolls.
         - scrollY moves from 0 to 800.
     - Page with html { overflow: hidden; }:
         - Wheel blocked.
         - scrollY remains 0.
     - This isolates the block to the overflow rule and proves the wheel mechanism itself works.

 4. Screenshot captured at 800×300:
     - Shows the clipped bottom.

 5. Caveat:
     - In this headless build, programmatic window.scrollTo bypasses the viewport’s overflow: hidden.
     - Observed scrollY moved from 0 to 150.
     - This is treated as a headless quirk, not valid page behavior.
     - User-initiated scrolling, specifically wheel, is the valid signal, and it is blocked.
     - In real browsers, the scrollbar simply never appears and wheel/trackpad/keyboard scrolling does nothing.

 ### Why LOW and Not MEDIUM

 - Trigger is an edge case:
     - Viewport inner height below approximately 292–299 px.
     - Small desktop windows.
     - Split panes.
     - Some low-resolution setups.
 - Consequence is limited:
     - The secondary date line becomes silently unreachable.
     - The lower portion of the clock face is clipped.
     - The primary time display remains visible for viewports approximately 160 px or taller.
 - Trivially worked around by resizing the window.
 - No data, security, or primary-workflow failure is identified.

 ### Required Correction

 Minimal robust fix:

 - Remove:
   ```css
     overflow: hidden;
   ```
   from the body rule at line 28.

 This allows the document to scroll normally whenever content exceeds the viewport.

 Alternative valid approaches, with more work:

 - Make the column fit short viewports.
 - Use responsive clock size, for example:
   ```css
     width: min(200px, 55vmin);
     height: min(200px, 55vmin);
   ```
 - Reduce gaps.
 - Add overflow-y: auto on html or body.

 The minimal recommended repair is deleting line 28. Nothing else on the page depends on that specific body overflow rule.

 ### Preservation Constraints

 - Keep:
   ```css
     .clock-face { overflow: hidden; }
   ```
   at line 65.
     - It intentionally clips hands to the circular face.
 - Keep:
   ```css
     min-height: 100vh;
     justify-content: center;
   ```
   so the layout remains centered on tall viewports.
 - Do not alter hand, face, or mark geometry.
 - Do not alter visual styling.
 - Do not conflate this fix with Finding 3’s missing viewport meta.
     - They are independent mechanisms:
         - Finding 3: mobile scale.
         - Finding 5: scroll clipping.
     - The two fixes are complementary, not conflicting.

 ### Post-Patch Verification

 1. Open the page in Chrome with a window inner height below approximately 300 px, for example 800×250.
     - The date line must be fully visible or reachable.
     - A scrollbar should appear, or content should fit.
     - Wheel/trackpad scrolling should reach the bottom.
     - Headless CDP check:
         - Dispatch mouseWheel with positive deltaY.
         - window.scrollY should increase.
         - date.getBoundingClientRect().bottom should become less than or equal to:
           ```js
             scrollY + innerHeight
           ```

 2. At 1280×800:
     - Layout must be pixel-identical to pre-patch.
     - Content remains centered.
     - No scrollbar appears.
     - Title, clock, and date positions are unchanged.

 3. Confirm the clock face still clips its hands:
     - Second-hand tip remains inside the face.
     - No visual change to the dial.

 Consolidated Repair Specification for Work Agent

 The following repair plan addresses all five confirmed findings while preserving the single-file, dependency-free nature of the page.

 Global Constraints

 - Modify only:
   ```text
     C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel\analog-clock\index_medium.html
   ```
 - Do not add external files, scripts, styles, fonts, or dependencies.
 - Preserve the page’s visual design on normal desktop viewports.
 - Preserve the 50 ms hand transition for normal per-second motion.
 - Preserve the 1-second boundary-aligned timer.
 - Preserve the forward-only rotation semantics of applyRotation, except for intentional transition suppression on large jumps.
 - Preserve .clock-face { overflow: hidden; }.
 - Keep accessibility changes visually inert.
 - Do not use per-second aria-live announcements.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Repair 1 — Normalize Hour Angle Before applyRotation

 Addresses: Finding 1
 Priority: Highest — MEDIUM functional defect.

 Change the hour base from:

 ```js
   const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12;
 ```

 to a 0–11 hour value:

 ```js
   const h12 = now.getHours() % 12;
 ```

 Then compute:

 ```js
   const hourDeg = (h12 / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;
 ```

 This ensures hourDeg is always in:

 ```text
   [0°, 360°)
 ```

 Do not change applyRotation for this repair.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Repair 2 — Add Visibility Handling and Suppress Large-Jump Animation

 Addresses: Finding 2
 Priority: LOW, but important for background-tab correctness.

 1. Store the pending timer ID.
 2. Replace direct setTimeout(tick, ...) usage with a single scheduling function that:
     - clears any existing pending timeout;
     - schedules exactly one new timeout;
     - stores the new timeout ID.
 3. Add:
    ```js
      document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => {
        if (document.visibilityState === 'visible') {
          updateClock();
          scheduleTick();
        }
      });
    ```
 4. In applyRotation, or a wrapper:
     - If the forward delta is greater than a threshold, for example 90°:
         - disable transition:
           ```js
             hand.style.transition = 'none';
           ```
         - set the transform;
         - force reflow:
           ```js
             void hand.offsetWidth;
           ```
         - restore the transition.
 5. Preserve normal 50 ms transitions for small deltas.

 This repair must not alter angle math beyond the Repair 1 normalization.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Repair 3 — Add Mobile Viewport Meta

 Addresses: Finding 3
 Priority: LOW.

 Add immediately after the charset meta:

 ```html
   <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
 ```

 Do not make the clock fluid unless separately required. The minimal fix is the meta tag only.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Repair 4 — Expose Time to Assistive Technology

 Addresses: Finding 4
 Priority: LOW.

 1. Change the clock container to include:
    ```html
      role="timer"
    ```
    Example:
    ```html
      <div class="clock" role="timer">
    ```
 2. In updateClock(), update the clock’s accessible label each tick:
    ```js
      clockEl.setAttribute('aria-label', now.toLocaleTimeString(undefined, {
        hour: 'numeric',
        minute: '2-digit',
        second: '2-digit'
      }));
    ```
 3. Optionally add a visually hidden text node updated each tick, but it must contribute zero layout.
 4. Do not add:
    ```html
      aria-live="polite"
    ```
    to the per-second time label.

 This repair must be visually inert.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Repair 5 — Remove overflow: hidden from body

 Addresses: Finding 5
 Priority: LOW.

 Remove line 28 from the body rule:

 ```css
   overflow: hidden;
 ```

 Keep the .clock-face overflow rule:

 ```css
   overflow: hidden;
 ```

 This makes the document scroll normally when content exceeds the viewport, while preserving centered layout on tall viewports.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Interaction and Conflict Notes

 - Finding 1 and Finding 2 interact.
     - Finding 1 normalizes the hour angle.
     - Finding 2 suppresses animation only for large catch-up jumps.
     - The transition suppression must not interfere with normal 0° or 0.5° hour-hand increments.

 - Finding 3 and Finding 5 are independent.
     - Finding 3 fixes mobile layout viewport scaling.
     - Finding 5 fixes desktop/short-window scroll reachability.
     - They are complementary and do not conflict.

 - Finding 4 is isolated.
     - It changes accessible semantics only.
     - It must not alter visual layout, timer behavior, angle math, or clipping.

 - Finding 5 must not remove .clock-face clipping.
     - Only the body overflow rule is the defect.
     - The circular face clipping is intentional.

 - All repairs preserve the single-file constraint.
     - No external CSS, JS, fonts, images, or libraries should be introduced.

 Non-Finding Residual Observations

 The following items appeared in the pass summaries as residual observations, rejected candidates, or cosmetic notes. They are not ranked findings and are not included in the required repair specification.

 Rejected Candidate

 - Unbounded setTimeout nesting depth:
     - Pass 2 simulated the timer chain and found the HTML-spec 4 ms minimum delay for deeply nested timers was never triggered.
     - Scheduled delay remained at least approximately 996 ms.
     - No user-visible effect was found.
     - Rejected as a defect.

 Accessibility-Adjacent Notes

 - The date is visible text, but the time was not accessible before Finding 4’s repair.
 - After Finding 4’s repair, the time should be exposed without per-second live announcements.

 Layout Notes

 - Short-viewport clipping was initially residual in earlier passes but was confirmed as Finding 5.
 - body { overflow: hidden; } was the confirmed mechanism for the short-viewport clipping.
 - .clock-face { overflow: hidden; } is intentional and should be preserved.

 Suspected But Unconfirmed

 - First-paint spin:
     - Hands may start at 0° before the inline script runs.
     - If the browser paints before script execution, the 50 ms transition could animate hands from 12 o’clock to the current time on load.
     - This was not confirmed in the passes and should not be treated as a required repair.

 Cosmetic/Maintainability Notes

 - File is named index_medium.html, not index.html.
     - It will not auto-load when the folder is opened in a browser.
 - <title> is “Analog Clock”.
 - <h1> is “Jack Kernel Demo”.
     - This is a cosmetic mismatch.
 - The .mark elements carry unused data-hour attributes.
     - Dead data, not a functional defect.
 - Unused CSS variables were noted:
     - --black
     - --black-soft
     - --diamond-shadow
 - The midnight hour-hand spin is the same root cause as the noon spin and is not a separate defect.

 Headless Runtime Caveat

 - In the headless Chrome build used for Finding 5, programmatic window.scrollTo bypassed the viewport’s overflow: hidden.
 - This is a headless quirk, not a page defect.
 - User-initiated wheel scrolling was the valid signal and was blocked.

 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

 Final Disposition

 This consolidated report confirms five supported defects:

 1. MEDIUM — Hour-hand full-spin loop during noon and midnight.
 2. LOW — Missing visibility/lifecycle handling and animated catch-up.
 3. LOW — Missing mobile viewport meta.
 4. LOW — No accessible time representation.
 5. LOW — body { overflow: hidden; } blocks short-viewport scrolling.

 No CRITICAL or HIGH severity findings were confirmed.

 The recommended repairs are mutually compatible, self-contained, and can be implemented in the single index_medium.html file without external dependencies.
